Sacrament</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sailors" class="mw-redirect" title="Sailors">sailors</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fishermen" class="mw-redirect" title="Fishermen">fishermen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Watermen" href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pila,_Laguna" title="Pila, Laguna">Pila, Laguna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iriga,_Camarines_Sur" class="mw-redirect" title="Iriga, Camarines Sur">Iriga, Camarines Sur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Camaligan,_Camarines_Sur" class="mw-redirect" title="Camaligan, Camarines Sur">Camaligan, Camarines Sur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gubat,_Sorsogon" class="mw-redirect" title="Gubat, Sorsogon">Gubat, Sorsogon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tuburan,_Cebu" title="Tuburan, Cebu">Tuburan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cebu" title="Cebu">Cebu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cusco" <p><b>Anthony of Padua</b>, <b><a href="/wiki/Order_of_Friars_Minor" title="Order of Friars Minor">OFM</a></b> (<a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>: <i lang="pt">António/Antônio de Pádua</i>, <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Antonio di/da Padova</i>, <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>: <i>Antōnius Patavīnus</i>) or <b>Anthony of Lisbon</b> (<a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>: <i lang="pt">António/Antônio de Lisboa</i>, <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Antonio da/di Lisbona</i>, <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>: <i>Antōnius Olisīpōnēnsis</i>, born <b>Fernando Martins de Bulhões</b>; 15 August 1195 – 13 June 1231)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPurcell196019,_275–276_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurcell196019,_275–276-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFoleyMcCloskey2013_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoleyMcCloskey2013-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> was a <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Portugal" title="Catholic Church in Portugal">Portuguese Catholic</a> priest and <a href="/wiki/Friar" title="Friar">friar</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Franciscan_Order" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan Order">Franciscan Order</a>.
</p><p>He was born and raised by a wealthy family in <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, and died in <a href="/wiki/Padua" title="Padua">Padua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>. Noted by his contemporaries for his powerful preaching, expert knowledge of scripture, and undying love and devotion to the poor and the sick, he was one of the most quickly <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> saints in church history, being canonized less than a year after his death.
</p><p>He was proclaimed a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctor of the Church</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> on 16 January 1946.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Life">Life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_years">Early years</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early years">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Igreja-Antonio1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Igreja-Antonio1.jpg/170px-Igreja-Antonio1.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="196" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Igreja-Antonio1.jpg/255px-Igreja-Antonio1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Igreja-Antonio1.jpg/340px-Igreja-Antonio1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1446" data-file-height="1668" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Church_of_Saint_Anthony_of_Lisbon" title="Church of Saint Anthony of Lisbon">Church of Saint Anthony</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, built over the original house where the Portuguese saint was born</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg/170px-La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="128" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg/255px-La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg/340px-La_casa_natale_di_S._Antonio_da_Padova_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a><figcaption>Birthplace of St. Anthony, in <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, located in the crypt of the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Saint_Anthony_of_Lisbon" title="Church of Saint Anthony of Lisbon">church dedicated to him</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Fernando Martins de Bulhões was born in <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> While 15th-century writers state that his parents were Vicente Martins and Teresa Pais Taveira, and that his father was the brother of Pedro Martins de Bulhões, the ancestor of the Bulhão or Bulhões family, Niccolò Dal-Gal views this as less certain.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> His wealthy and noble family arranged for him to be instructed at the local <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_school" title="Cathedral school">cathedral school</a>. At the age of 15, he entered the <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_St._Augustine" class="mw-redirect" title="Rule of St. Augustine">Augustinian</a> community of <a href="/wiki/Canons_Regular_of_the_Holy_Cross_of_Coimbra" title="Canons Regular of the Holy Cross of Coimbra">Canons Regular of the Order of the Holy Cross</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Monastery_of_S%C3%A3o_Vicente_de_Fora" title="Monastery of São Vicente de Fora">Abbey of Saint Vincent</a> on the outskirts of Lisbon.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1212, distracted by frequent visits from family and friends, he asked to be transferred to the <a href="/wiki/Motherhouse" title="Motherhouse">motherhouse</a> of the congregation, the <a href="/wiki/Monastery_of_the_Holy_Cross_(Coimbra)" title="Monastery of the Holy Cross (Coimbra)">Monastery of the Holy Cross</a> in <a href="/wiki/Coimbra" title="Coimbra">Coimbra</a>, then the capital of Portugal.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonti2008_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonti2008-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> There, the young Fernando studied <a href="/wiki/Theology" title="Theology">theology</a> and <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Joining_the_Franciscans">Joining the Franciscans</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Joining the Franciscans">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Accademia_-_Madonna_in_trono_con_il_Bambino_tra_i_santi_Anna,_Gioachino,_Ludovico_da_Tolosa,_Antoinio_da_Padova,_Francesco_et_Bernardino_da_Siena_-_Alvise_Vivarini_Cat607.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Accademia_-_Madonna_in_trono_con_il_Bambino_tra_i_santi_Anna%2C_Gioachino%2C_Ludovico_da_Tolosa%2C_Antoinio_da_Padova%2C_Francesco_et_Bernardino_da_Siena_-_Alvise_Vivarini_Cat607.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="206" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Accademia_-_Madonna_in_trono_con_il_Bambino_tra_i_santi_Anna%2C_Gioachino%2C_Ludovico_da_Tolosa%2C_Antoinio_da_Padova%2C_Francesco_et_Bernardino_da_Siena_-_Alvise_Vivarini_Cat607.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Accademia_-_Madonna_in_trono_con_il_Bambino_tra_i_santi_Anna%2C_Gioachino%2C_Ludovico_da_Tolosa%2C_Antoinio_da_Padova%2C_Francesco_et_Bernardino_da_Siena_-_Alvise_Vivarini_Cat607.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4598" data-file-height="4301" /></a><figcaption>In <a href="/wiki/Alvise_Vivarini" title="Alvise Vivarini">Alvise Vivarini</a>'s painting,  Anthony is distinguished from the other saints by his attributes: the book and the white lily stalk.</figcaption></figure>
<p>After his <a href="/wiki/Ordination" title="Ordination">ordination</a> to the priesthood, Fernando was named guestmaster at the age of 19, and placed in charge of hospitality for the <a href="/wiki/Abbey" title="Abbey">abbey</a>. While he was in Coimbra, some <a href="/wiki/Franciscans" title="Franciscans">Franciscan</a> <a href="/wiki/Friar" title="Friar">friars</a> arrived and settled at a small <a href="/wiki/Hermitage_(religious_retreat)" title="Hermitage (religious retreat)">hermitage</a> outside Coimbra dedicated to <a href="/wiki/Anthony_the_Great" title="Anthony the Great">Anthony the Great</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonti2008_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonti2008-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Fernando was strongly attracted to the simple, <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_counsels" title="Evangelical counsels">evangelical</a> lifestyle of the friars, whose <a href="/wiki/Religious_order_(Catholic)" title="Religious order (Catholic)">order</a> had been founded only 11 years prior. News arrived that five Franciscans had been beheaded in <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Morocco" title="Catholic Church in Morocco">Morocco</a>, <a href="/wiki/Franciscan_protomartyrs" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan protomartyrs">the first of their order to be killed</a>. King <a href="/wiki/Afonso_II_of_Portugal" title="Afonso II of Portugal">Afonso II of Portugal</a> ransomed their bodies to be returned and buried as <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyrs</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Monastery_of_the_Holy_Cross_(Coimbra)" title="Monastery of the Holy Cross (Coimbra)">Monastery of the Holy Cross</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMonti2008_4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMonti2008-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Inspired by their example, Fernando obtained permission from church authorities to leave the <a href="/wiki/Canons_Regular" class="mw-redirect" title="Canons Regular">Canons Regular</a> to join the new Franciscan order. Upon his admission to the life of the friars, he joined the small hermitage in <a href="/wiki/Santo_Ant%C3%B3nio_dos_Olivais" title="Santo António dos Olivais">Olivais</a>, adopting the name Anthony (from the name of the chapel located there, dedicated to Anthony the Great), by which he was to be known.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESilva20111_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESilva20111-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Anthony then set out for Morocco, in fulfilment of his new <a href="/wiki/Vocation" title="Vocation">vocation</a>. However, he fell seriously ill in Morocco and set sail back for Portugal in hope of regaining his health. On the return voyage, the ship was pushed off course and landed in <a href="/wiki/Sicily" title="Sicily">Sicily</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-wintz_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wintz-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From Sicily, he made his way to <a href="/wiki/Tuscany" title="Tuscany">Tuscany</a>, where he was assigned to a <a href="/wiki/Convent" title="Convent">convent</a> of the order, but he was met with difficulty on account of his sickly appearance. He was finally assigned to the rural hermitage of <a href="/wiki/San_Paolo" title="San Paolo">San Paolo</a> near <a href="/wiki/Forl%C3%AC" title="Forlì">Forlì</a>, <a href="/wiki/Romagna" title="Romagna">Romagna</a>, a choice made after considering his poor health. There, he had recourse to a cell one of the friars had made in a nearby cave, spending time in private prayer and study.<sup id="cite_ref-foley_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foley-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Preaching_and_teaching">Preaching and teaching</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Preaching and teaching">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg/170px-Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="198" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg/255px-Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg/340px-Saint_Antony_of_Padua_holding_Baby_Jesus_mg_0165.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2527" data-file-height="2941" /></a><figcaption><i><a href="/wiki/Saint_Anthony_of_Padua_holding_the_Infant_Jesus" title="Saint Anthony of Padua holding the Infant Jesus">Saint Anthony of Padua holding the Infant Jesus</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Bernardo_Strozzi" title="Bernardo Strozzi">Strozzi</a>, c. 1625; the white <a href="/wiki/Lilium" title="Lilium">lily</a> represents purity.</figcaption></figure> 
<p>In 1222, in the town of <a href="/wiki/Forl%C3%AC" title="Forlì">Forlì</a>, a number of visiting <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Dominican</a> friars were present for the occasion of an ordination, and a misunderstanding arose over who should preach. The Franciscans had expected that one of the Dominicans would occupy the pulpit, being renowned for their preaching. However, the Dominicans had come unprepared, thinking a Franciscan would be the <a href="/wiki/Homily" title="Homily">homilist</a>. In this quandary, the head of the hermitage, who did not think any of his own humble friars could give a homily for the occasion, called upon Anthony, whom he suspected was most qualified, and entreated him to speak whatever the Holy Spirit might inspire.<sup id="cite_ref-wintz_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wintz-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Anthony objected, but was overruled, and his impromptu sermon created a deep impression on his audience. His audience was moved not only by his rich voice and arresting manner, but also by the theme and substance of his discourse, his deep knowledge of scripture, and the eloquence with which he delivered his message.
</p><p>Anthony was then sent by Brother Gratian, the local <a href="/wiki/Provincial_superior" title="Provincial superior">minister provincial</a>, to the Franciscan province of <a href="/wiki/Romagna" title="Romagna">Romagna</a>, based in <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-wintz_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wintz-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> He soon came to the attention of the founder of the order, <a href="/wiki/Francis_of_Assisi" title="Francis of Assisi">Francis of Assisi</a>. Francis had held a strong distrust of the place of <a href="/wiki/Theology" title="Theology">theological studies</a> in the life of his <a href="/wiki/Franciscans" title="Franciscans">brotherhood</a>, fearing it might lead to an abandonment of their commitment to a life of real poverty and service. In Anthony, however, he found a kindred spirit who shared his vision and could also provide the teaching any young members of the order seeking ordination might need. In 1224, he entrusted the pursuit of studies for any of his friars to the care of Anthony.
</p><p>The traditional practice of praying for St. Anthony's help in finding lost or stolen things is traced to an incident during his lifetime that occurred in Bologna. According to the story, Anthony had a <a href="/wiki/Psalter" title="Psalter">book of psalms</a> that was important to him, as it contained his notes and comments for use in teaching his students. A <a href="/wiki/Novitiate" title="Novitiate">novice</a> who had chosen to leave had taken the psalter with him. Prior to the <a href="/wiki/Movable_type" title="Movable type">invention of the printing press</a>, any book was <a href="/wiki/Illuminated_manuscript" title="Illuminated manuscript">hand-copied</a>, and thus, an item of high value; a Franciscan friar in particular, given his vow of poverty, would have found such an item difficult to replace. When Anthony realized his psalter was missing, he prayed it would be found or returned, after which the thief was moved to not only return the book to Anthony, but also return to the order. The stolen book is said to be preserved in the Franciscan friary in Bologna.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Occasionally, Anthony took another post as a teacher at universities like <a href="/wiki/University_of_Montpellier" title="University of Montpellier">University of Montpellier</a> and <a href="/wiki/University_of_Toulouse" title="University of Toulouse">University of Toulouse</a> in southern France, but his preaching was considered to be his supreme gift. According to historian Sophronius Clasen, Anthony preached "the grandeur of Christianity".<sup id="cite_ref-foley_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foley-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> His method included <a href="/wiki/Allegory" title="Allegory">allegory</a> and symbolical explanation of scripture. In 1226, after attending the <a href="/wiki/Chapter_(religion)" title="Chapter (religion)">general chapter</a> of his order held at <a href="/wiki/Arles" title="Arles">Arles</a>, France, and preaching in the region of <a href="/wiki/Provence" title="Provence">Provence</a>, Anthony returned to Italy and was appointed <a href="/wiki/Provincial_superior" title="Provincial superior">Provincial superior</a> of northern Italy. He chose the city of Padua as his location.
</p><p>In 1228, he served as <a href="/wiki/Envoy_(title)" title="Envoy (title)">envoy</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Chapter_(religion)" title="Chapter (religion)">general chapter</a> to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gregory_IX" title="Pope Gregory IX">Pope Gregory IX</a>. At the <a href="/wiki/Papal_household" title="Papal household">papal court</a>, his preaching was hailed as a "jewel case of the <a href="/wiki/Bible" title="Bible">Bible</a>" and he was commissioned to produce his collection of sermons, <i>Sermons for Feast Days</i> <i>(Sermones in Festivitates)</i>. Gregory IX described Anthony as the "<a href="/wiki/Ark_of_the_Covenant" title="Ark of the Covenant">Ark</a> of the Testament" <i>(Doctor Arca testamenti)</i>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Miracles">Miracles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Miracles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_(c._1630)_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_(Detroit_Institute_of_Arts).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_%28c._1630%29_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_%28Detroit_Institute_of_Arts%29.png/220px-St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_%28c._1630%29_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_%28Detroit_Institute_of_Arts%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="201" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_%28c._1630%29_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_%28Detroit_Institute_of_Arts%29.png/330px-St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_%28c._1630%29_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_%28Detroit_Institute_of_Arts%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_%28c._1630%29_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_%28Detroit_Institute_of_Arts%29.png/440px-St._Anthony_Preaching_to_the_Fishes_%28c._1630%29_-_attributed_to_Francisco_de_Herrera_the_Elder_%28Detroit_Institute_of_Arts%29.png 2x" data-file-width="1726" data-file-height="1578" /></a><figcaption><i>Saint Anthony Preaching to the Fishes</i>, c. 1630</figcaption></figure>
<p>The story of Anthony "preaching to the fish" originated in <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a>, where he had gone to preach. When <a href="/wiki/Heresy" title="Heresy">heretics</a> there treated him with contempt, Anthony was said to have gone to the shoreline, where he began to preach at the water's edge until a great crowd of fish was seen gathered before him. The people of the town flocked to see this marvel, after which Anthony charged them with the fact that the fish were more receptive to his message than the heretics of the church, at which point the people were moved to listen to his message.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEArnald2010122–125_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEArnald2010122–125-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In another often-told story, which took place also in Rimini, Anthony was challenged by a heretic to prove the <a href="/wiki/Real_presence_of_Christ_in_the_Eucharist" title="Real presence of Christ in the Eucharist">reality of the presence of Christ in the Eucharist</a>. The man, who sought to mock Anthony, brought out a half-starved <a href="/wiki/Mule" title="Mule">mule</a> and showed it fresh <a href="/wiki/Fodder" title="Fodder">fodder</a>. Saint Anthony showed the mule the <a href="/wiki/Monstrance" title="Monstrance">monstrance</a> containing the Blessed Sacrament. The mule ignored the fodder and bowed before the Blessed Sacrament.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEArnald2010122–125_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEArnald2010122–125-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Tempietto_of_Sant%27Antonio,_Rimini" title="Tempietto of Sant&#39;Antonio, Rimini">Tempietto of Sant'Antonio</a> marks the spot where the miracle took place.  Other versions of this miracle place it in <a href="/wiki/Toulouse" title="Toulouse">Toulouse</a> or <a href="/wiki/Bourges" title="Bourges">Bourges</a>.
</p><p>Another account tells of an occasion in Italy when Anthony was dining with heretics. He realized the food they put before him was poisoned, and he confronted them. The men admitted to attempting to kill him, but then challenged him to eat if he truly believed the words spoken in Mark 16:18 about the apostles of Christ: "…and if they drink any deadly thing, it will not harm them." Anthony is said to have blessed the food, eaten it, and suffered no harm, much to the amazement of his hosts.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEArnald2010122–125_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEArnald2010122–125-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Anthony_pereda.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Anthony_pereda.jpg/220px-Anthony_pereda.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/df/Anthony_pereda.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="317" data-file-height="324" /></a><figcaption><i>Anthony of Padua with the Infant Jesus</i> by <a href="/wiki/Antonio_de_Pereda" title="Antonio de Pereda">Antonio de Pereda</a>, detail</figcaption></figure>
<p>Anthony became sick with <a href="/wiki/Ergotism" title="Ergotism">ergotism</a> in 1231 and went to the woodland retreat at <a href="/wiki/Camposampiero" title="Camposampiero">Camposampiero</a> with two other friars for a respite. There, he lived in a room built for him under the branches of a walnut tree. Anthony died on the way back to Padua on 13 June 1231 at the <a href="/wiki/Poor_Clare" class="mw-redirect" title="Poor Clare">Poor Clare</a> monastery at <a href="/wiki/Arcella_(Padua)" title="Arcella (Padua)">Arcella</a> (now part of Padua), at the age of 35.
</p><p>According to his request, Anthony was buried in the small church of Santa Maria Mater Domini (probably dating from the late 12th century) and near a convent that had been founded by him in 1229. Nevertheless, due to his increased notability, the construction of a large <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Anthony_of_Padua" title="Basilica of Saint Anthony of Padua">basilica</a> began around 1232, although it was not completed until 1301. The smaller church was incorporated into the structure as the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Anthony_of_Padua" title="Basilica of Saint Anthony of Padua">Cappella della Madonna Mora</a> (Chapel of the Dark Madonna). The basilica is commonly known today as "Il Santo" (The Saint).
</p><p>Various legends also surround the death of Anthony. One holds that when he died, children cried in the streets and all the <a href="/wiki/Church_bell" title="Church bell">church bells</a> rang of their own accord. Another legend is in regard to his tongue. He was buried in a chapel that became part of the <a href="/wiki/Basilica" title="Basilica">basilica</a>, where his tongue, jaw, and vocal cords were symbolically chosen as <a href="/wiki/Relics" class="mw-redirect" title="Relics">relics</a> for <a href="/wiki/Veneration" title="Veneration">veneration</a> (as was a tradition in medieval times) to be displayed in a large <a href="/wiki/Reliquary" title="Reliquary">reliquary</a>. When his body was exhumed 30 years after his death, it was found turned to dust, but the tongue was claimed to have glistened and looked as if it were still part of a live body; a further claim being made that this was a sign of his gift of preaching.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> On 1 January 1981, Pope <a href="/wiki/John_Paul_II" class="mw-redirect" title="John Paul II">John Paul II</a> authorized a scientific team to study Anthony's remains and the tomb was opened five days later.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Saint_and_Doctor_of_the_Church">Saint and Doctor of the Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Saint and Doctor of the Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg/220px-Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="129" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg/330px-Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg/440px-Friedrich_Pacher_-_St_Anthony_of_Padua_and_St_Francis_of_Assisi_-_WGA16806.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1109" data-file-height="650" /></a><figcaption><i>St Anthony of Padua and St Francis of Assisi</i> by Friedrich Pacher</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Basilica of Saint Anthony of Padua -Padua, Italy" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg/220px-Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="99" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg/330px-Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg/440px-Basilica_de_Sant%27Antonio_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5682" data-file-height="2558" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Anthony_of_Padua" title="Basilica of Saint Anthony of Padua">Basilica of Saint Anthony of Padua</a> - Padua, Italy</figcaption></figure>
<p>Anthony was <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> by Pope Gregory IX on 30 May 1232, at <a href="/wiki/Spoleto" title="Spoleto">Spoleto</a>, Italy, less than one year after his death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDal-Gal1907-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> In the words of Pope <a href="/wiki/Benedict_XVI" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedict XVI">Benedict XVI</a>:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The richness of spiritual teaching contained in the <i>Sermons</i> was so great that in 1946 <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> proclaimed Anthony a Doctor of the Church, attributing to him the title <i>Doctor evangelicus</i>, since the freshness and beauty of the Gospel emerge from these writings.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration_as_patron_saint">Veneration as patron saint</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Veneration as patron saint">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Anthony's fame spread through Portuguese evangelization, and he has been known as the most celebrated of the followers of Francis of Assisi. He is the patron saint of Padua and many places in <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a> and in the countries of the former <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Empire" title="Portuguese Empire">Portuguese Empire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He is especially invoked and venerated all over the world as the patron saint for the recovery of lost items and is credited with many miracles involving lost people, lost things and even lost spiritual goods.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>St. Anthony Chaplets help devotees to meditate on the thirteen virtues of the saint. Some of these chaplets were used by members of confraternities that had Anthony as their patron saint.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="North_America">North America</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: North America">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_(Cincinnati,_Ohio)_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_%28Cincinnati%2C_Ohio%29_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg/220px-National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_%28Cincinnati%2C_Ohio%29_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_%28Cincinnati%2C_Ohio%29_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg/330px-National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_%28Cincinnati%2C_Ohio%29_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_%28Cincinnati%2C_Ohio%29_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg/440px-National_Shrine_of_St._Anthony_and_Friary_%28Cincinnati%2C_Ohio%29_-_first_class_relic_of_St._Anthony.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3251" data-file-height="3253" /></a><figcaption>First class relic of Anthony displayed at the National Shrine of St. Anthony of Padua and Friary (<a href="/wiki/Cincinnati,_Ohio" class="mw-redirect" title="Cincinnati, Ohio">Cincinnati, Ohio</a>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1692, Spanish missionaries came across a small <a href="/wiki/Payaya_Indians" class="mw-redirect" title="Payaya Indians">Payaya Indian</a> community along what was then known as the <a href="/wiki/Yanaguana_(San_Antonio)" title="Yanaguana (San Antonio)">Yanaguana</a> River on the feast day of Saint Anthony, 13 June. The Franciscan chaplain, Father Damien Massanet, with agreement from General Domingo de Teran, renamed the rivers in his honor, and eventually built <a href="/wiki/Alamo_Mission_in_San_Antonio" class="mw-redirect" title="Alamo Mission in San Antonio">a mission nearby</a>, as well. This mission became the focal point of a small community that eventually grew in size and scope to become the seventh-largest city in the country, the <a href="/wiki/San_Antonio" title="San Antonio">U.S. city of San Antonio, Texas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York City</a>, the <a href="/wiki/St._Anthony_of_Padua_Church_(Manhattan)" title="St. Anthony of Padua Church (Manhattan)">Shrine Church of St. Anthony</a> in <a href="/wiki/Greenwich_Village" title="Greenwich Village">Greenwich Village</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manhattan" title="Manhattan">Manhattan</a> celebrates his <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">feast day</a>, starting with the traditional <a href="/wiki/Novena" title="Novena">novena</a> of prayers asking for his intercession on the 13 Tuesdays preceding his feast. This culminates with a week-long series of services and a street fair. A traditional Italian-style procession is held that day through the streets of its <a href="/wiki/South_Village" title="South Village">South Village</a> neighborhood, during which a <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">relic</a> of the saint is carried for <a href="/wiki/Veneration" title="Veneration">veneration</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg/220px-Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg/330px-Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg/440px-Cudowny_Obraz_Swietego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1932" data-file-height="2576" /></a><figcaption>Miraculous Image of Saint Anthony, by Franciszek Lekszycki OFM, 1649, <a href="/wiki/Przeworsk" title="Przeworsk">Przeworsk</a>, Poland</figcaption></figure>
<p>Each year on the weekend of the last Sunday in August, <a href="/wiki/North_End,_Boston" title="North End, Boston">Boston's North End</a> holds a feast in honor of Saint Anthony. Referred to as the "Feast of All Feasts", <a href="/wiki/Feast_of_St._Anthony" title="Feast of St. Anthony">Saint Anthony's Feast</a> in Boston's North End was begun in 1919 by Italian immigrants from <a href="/wiki/Montefalcione" title="Montefalcione">Montefalcione</a>, a small town near <a href="/wiki/Naples" title="Naples">Naples</a>, where the tradition of honoring Saint Anthony goes back to 1688.<sup id="cite_ref-Aluia_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Aluia-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_(cropped).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="279" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-Broadsheet_with_image_of_Saint_Antony_of_Padua_MET_DP867949_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2356" data-file-height="2986" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Guadalupe_Posada" title="José Guadalupe Posada">José Guadalupe Posada</a> print of Saint Anthony, about 1910</figcaption></figure>
<p>Each year the <a href="/wiki/Sandia_Pueblo" title="Sandia Pueblo">Sandia Pueblo</a> along with <a href="/wiki/Santa_Clara_Pueblo" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Clara Pueblo">Santa Clara Pueblo</a> celebrates the feast day of Saint Anthony with traditional Native American dances.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESweet2004_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESweet2004-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 27 January 1907, in <a href="/wiki/Beaumont,_Texas" title="Beaumont, Texas">Beaumont, Texas</a>, a church was dedicated and named in honor of Saint Anthony. The church was later designated a cathedral in 1966 with the formation of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Beaumont" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Beaumont">Roman Catholic Diocese of Beaumont</a>, but was not formally consecrated. On 28 April 1974, St. Anthony Cathedral was dedicated and consecrated by Bishop Warren Boudreaux. In 2006, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> granted the cathedral the designation of minor basilica. <a href="/wiki/Saint_Anthony_Cathedral_Basilica_in_Beaumont,_Texas" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Anthony Cathedral Basilica in Beaumont, Texas">St. Anthony Cathedral Basilica</a> celebrated its 100th anniversary on 28 January 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-thc_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thc-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>St. Anthony gives his name to <a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Antonio_de_Padua" title="Mission San Antonio de Padua">Mission San Antonio de Padua</a>, the third Franciscan mission dedicated along <a href="/wiki/El_Camino_Real_(California)" title="El Camino Real (California)">El Camino Real</a> in <a href="/wiki/California" title="California">California</a> in 1771.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/Ellicott_City,_Maryland" title="Ellicott City, Maryland">Ellicott City, Maryland</a>, southwest of <a href="/wiki/Baltimore" title="Baltimore">Baltimore</a>, the Conventual Franciscans of the St. Anthony Province dedicated their old novitiate house as the <a href="/wiki/Shrine_of_St._Anthony_(Maryland)" title="Shrine of St. Anthony (Maryland)">Shrine of St. Anthony</a> which since 1 July 2004 serves as the official shrine to Saint Anthony for the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Baltimore" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Baltimore">Archdiocese of Baltimore</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BaltSun_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BaltSun-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Portugal.2C_Spain_and_Brazil"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Portugal,_Spain_and_Brazil">Portugal, Spain and Brazil</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Portugal, Spain and Brazil">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio,_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio%2C_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg/220px-Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio%2C_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio%2C_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg/330px-Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio%2C_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio%2C_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg/440px-Igreja_de_Santo_Ant%C3%B4nio%2C_Teres%C3%B3polis.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a><figcaption>Santo Antônio (Saint Anthony) Church in <a href="/wiki/Teres%C3%B3polis" title="Teresópolis">Teresópolis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Saint Anthony is known in Portugal, Spain, and Brazil as a marriage saint, because legends exist of him reconciling couples. His feast day, 13 June, is Lisbon's municipal holiday, celebrated with parades and marriages (the previous day, 12 June, is the <i><a href="/wiki/Dia_dos_Namorados" title="Dia dos Namorados">Dia dos Namorados</a></i> in Brazil). He is one of the saints celebrated in the Brazilian <i><a href="/wiki/Festa_Junina" title="Festa Junina">Festa Junina</a></i>, along with <a href="/wiki/John_the_Baptist" title="John the Baptist">John the Baptist</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Saint Peter</a>. He is venerated in Mogán Village in <a href="/wiki/Gran_Canaria" title="Gran Canaria">Gran Canaria</a>, where his feast day is celebrated every year with oversized objects carried through the streets for the fiesta.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_rest_of_Europe">The rest of Europe</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: The rest of Europe">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre,_Belgium_(DSCF7546).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre%2C_Belgium_%28DSCF7546%29.jpg/220px-Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre%2C_Belgium_%28DSCF7546%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="177" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre%2C_Belgium_%28DSCF7546%29.jpg/330px-Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre%2C_Belgium_%28DSCF7546%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre%2C_Belgium_%28DSCF7546%29.jpg/440px-Paroisse_Saint-Antoine_de_Padoue_in_Wavre%2C_Belgium_%28DSCF7546%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3989" data-file-height="3218" /></a><figcaption>Saint-Antoine de Padoue parish in Wavre, Belgium</figcaption></figure>
<p>In <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Franciscans" title="Franciscans">Franciscans</a> arrived in 1240 spreading the word of Saint Anthony <i>(Shna Ndou)</i>. The <a href="/wiki/St._Anthony_Church,_La%C3%A7" title="St. Anthony Church, Laç">St. Anthony Church, Laç</a> (<a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian</a>: <i lang="sq">Kisha e Shna Ndout or Kisha e Laçit</i>) in <a href="/wiki/La%C3%A7" title="Laç">Laç</a> was built in his honor.<sup id="cite_ref-Merlika_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Merlika-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>Saint Anthony is one of the most important saints amongst Albanians and the pilgrimage to his church is done yearly by a considerable number of <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Albania" title="Religion in Albania">non-catholic Albanians</a> as well.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the town of <a href="/wiki/Brusciano" title="Brusciano">Brusciano</a>, Italy, located near Naples, an annual feast in honor of Saint Anthony is held in late August. This tradition dates back to 1875. The tradition started when a man prayed to Saint Anthony for his sick son to get better. He vowed that if his son would become healthy he would build and dance a <i>giglio</i> like the people of Nola do for their patron San Paolino during the annual Fest Dei Gigli. (A <i>giglio</i> is a tall tower topped with a statue of the saint that is carried through the streets in carefully choreographed maneuvers that resemble a dance.) The celebration has grown over the years to include six giglio towers built in honor of the saint. This tradition has also carried over to America, specifically the <a href="/wiki/East_Harlem" title="East Harlem">East Harlem</a> area of New York, where the immigrants from the town of Brusciano formed the <a href="/wiki/Giglio_Society_of_East_Harlem" title="Giglio Society of East Harlem">Giglio Society of East Harlem</a> and have been holding their annual feast since the early 1900s.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/Poland" title="Poland">Poland</a>, he is the patron saint of <a href="/wiki/Przeworsk" title="Przeworsk">Przeworsk</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Icon" title="Icon">icon</a> of Saint Anthony, dating from 1649, is housed in a local Franciscan church, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Kaplica_%C5%9Awi%C4%99tego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Kaplica Świętego Antoniego w Przeworsku (page does not exist)">Kaplica Świętego Antoniego w Przeworsku</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://pl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaplica_%C5%9Awi%C4%99tego_Antoniego_w_Przeworsku" class="extiw" title="pl:Kaplica Świętego Antoniego w Przeworsku">pl</a>&#93;</span>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Asia">Asia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Asia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Saint Anthony is honored in <a href="/wiki/Uvari" title="Uvari">Uvari</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a>, India, where legend holds that the crew of a Portuguese ship was cured of cholera through the intercession of St. Anthony. The local church is dedicated to St. Anthony and houses an ancient wooden statue believed to have been carved by one of the crew.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERaj2017_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERaj2017-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>, the devotion to St. Anthony of Padua began in 1581, in the town of <a href="/wiki/Pila,_Laguna" title="Pila, Laguna">Pila, Laguna</a>, where Franciscans established the first church in the country dedicated to St. Anthony of Padua, now elevated as the <a href="/wiki/Pila_Church" title="Pila Church">National Shrine of St. Anthony of Padua</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_San_Pablo" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of San Pablo">Diocese of San Pablo</a>.
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/Siolim" title="Siolim">Siolim</a>, a village in the Indian state of <a href="/wiki/Goa" title="Goa">Goa</a>, St. Anthony is always shown holding a serpent on a stick. This is a depiction of the incident which occurred during the construction of the church wherein a snake was disrupting construction work. The people turned to St. Anthony for help and placed his statue at the construction site. The next morning, the snake was found caught in the cord placed in the statue's hand.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-traditional">
		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 180px">
			<div class="thumb" style="width: 175px; height: 230px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="St. Anthony of Padua in St. Joseph&#39;s Church, Macao"><img alt="St. Anthony of Padua in St. Joseph&#39;s Church, Macao" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg/130px-St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg" decoding="async" width="130" height="200" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg/195px-St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg/260px-St._Anthony_of_Padua.jpg 2x" data-file-width="667" data-file-height="1024" /></a></span></div>

</p>

		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 180px">
			<div class="thumb" style="width: 175px; height: 230px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:JC_Pila_57.JPG" class="mw-file-description" title="National Shrine of St. Anthony of Padua, Pila, Laguna, Philippines where Franciscans established the first church in the country dedicated to St. Anthony of Padua under the Diocese of San Pablo"><img alt="National Shrine of St. Anthony of Padua, Pila, Laguna, Philippines where Franciscans established the first church in the country dedicated to St. Anthony of Padua under the Diocese of San Pablo" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/JC_Pila_57.JPG/133px-JC_Pila_57.JPG" decoding="async" width="133" height="200" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/JC_Pila_57.JPG/200px-JC_Pila_57.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/JC_Pila_57.JPG/266px-JC_Pila_57.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1680" data-file-height="2520" /></a></span></div>

<p><a href="/wiki/Pila_Church" title="Pila Church">National Shrine of St. Anthony of Padua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pila,_Laguna" title="Pila, Laguna">Pila, Laguna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> where Franciscans established the first church in the country dedicated to St. Anthony of Padua under the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_San_Pablo" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of San Pablo">Diocese of San Pablo</a>
</p>
			</div>
		</li>
</ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Africa">Africa</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Africa">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Antonianism" title="Antonianism">Antonianism</a> (<a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>: <i lang="pt">antonianismo</i>) was a <a href="/wiki/Syncretic" class="mw-redirect" title="Syncretic">syncretic</a> <a href="/wiki/Kongo_people" title="Kongo people">Bakongo</a> <a href="/wiki/Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic">Catholic movement</a> formed in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Kongo" title="Kingdom of Kongo">Kingdom of Kongo</a> between 1704 and 1708 as a development out of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church_in_Kongo" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church in Kongo">Roman Catholic Church in Kongo</a>, yet without denying the authority of the <a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a>. Its founder was a young charismatic woman named <a href="/wiki/Kimpa_Vita" title="Kimpa Vita">Beatriz Kimpa Vita</a> who said she was possessed by Saint Anthony of Padua.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStearns2001394_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStearns2001394-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThornton1998_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThornton1998-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Beatriz became known for healing and other miracles. It was eventually suppressed by King <a href="/wiki/Pedro_IV_of_Kongo" title="Pedro IV of Kongo">Pedro IV of Kongo</a>, and Dona Beatriz was burned at the stake as a heretic.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWessinger2011391_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWessinger2011391-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_art">In art</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: In art">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>As the number of Franciscan saints increased, iconography struggled to distinguish Anthony from the others. Because of a legend that he had once preached to the fish by the mouth of the river <a href="/wiki/Marecchia" title="Marecchia">Marecchia</a> in <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a>, this was sometimes used as his attribute. He is also often seen with a white <a href="/wiki/Lilium" title="Lilium">lily</a> stalk, representing his purity. Other conventions referred to St. Anthony's visionary fervor. Thus, one attribute in use for some time was a flaming heart. He is also sometimes depicted along with the <a href="/wiki/Mule" title="Mule">mule</a> in Rimini that allegedly bowed down to him holding the <a href="/wiki/Eucharist" title="Eucharist">Eucharist</a>.
</p><p>In 1511, <a href="/wiki/Titian" title="Titian">Titian</a> painted three large frescoes in the <a href="/wiki/Scuola_del_Santo" title="Scuola del Santo">Scuola del Santo</a> in Padua, depicting scenes of the miracles from the life of Saint Anthony: <i>The <a href="/wiki/Miracle_of_the_Jealous_Husband" title="Miracle of the Jealous Husband">Miracle of the Jealous Husband</a></i>, which depicts the murder of a young woman by her husband; <i>A Child Testifying to Its Mother's Innocence</i>; and <i>The Saint Healing the Young Man with a Broken Limb</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMorosini1999163–164_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorosini1999163–164-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Another key pattern has him meditating on an open book in which the Christ Child himself appears, as in the El Greco above. Over time the child came to be shown considerably larger than the book and some images even do without the book entirely. He typically appears carrying the infant Jesus and holding a cross.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChong2016189_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChong2016189-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In popular <a href="/wiki/Votive_offering" title="Votive offering">votive offerings</a>, Anthony is often depicted as miraculously saving individuals in various accidents.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_music">In music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: In music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Austrian composer <a href="/wiki/Gustav_Mahler" title="Gustav Mahler">Gustav Mahler</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Song_cycle" title="Song cycle">song cycle</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Des_Knaben_Wunderhorn" title="Des Knaben Wunderhorn">Des Knaben Wunderhorn</a></i> contains the song <i>Des Antonius von Padua Fischpredigt</i>, whose lyrics recount the story of Saint Anthony's sermon to the fish. This song later formed the basis for the scherzo movement of Mahler's <a href="/wiki/Symphony_No._2_(Mahler)" title="Symphony No. 2 (Mahler)">Symphony No. 2</a>. In correspondence, Mahler expressed amusement that his sinuous musical setting could imply <a href="/wiki/St._Anthony_of_Padua" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Anthony of Padua">St. Anthony of Padua</a> was himself drunk as he preached to the fish.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_films">In films</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Anthony_of_Padua&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: In films">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>The 1931 silent film <i>Saint Anthony of Padua</i> <i>(Antonio di Padova, il santo dei miracoli)</i> was directed by <a href="/wiki/Giulio_Antamoro" title="Giulio Antamoro">Giulio Antamoro</a>.</li>
<li>He was played in the 1949 Italian film <i><a href="/wiki/Anthony_of_Padua_(film)" title="Anthony of Padua (film)">Anthony of Padua</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Aldo_Fiorelli" title="Aldo Fiorelli">Aldo Fiorelli</a>.</li>
<li><i>Punitha Anthoniyar</i> <i>(Saint Anthony)</i>, a 1977 Indian <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a>-language film directed by Nanjil Durai, starring <a href="/wiki/R._Muthuraman" title="R. Muthuraman">R. Muthuraman</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lakshmi_(actress)" title="Lakshmi (actress)">Lakshmi</a> in the lead roles.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umberto_Marino" class="extiw" title="it:Umberto Marino">Umberto Marino</a>'s 2002 <i>Sant'Antonio di Padova</i> or <i>Saint Anthony: The Miracle Worker of Padua</i> is an Italian <a href="/wiki/Television_film" title="Television film">TV movie</a> about the saint.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> While the VHS format is without English subtitles,<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> the DVD version released in 2005 is simply called <i>Saint Anthony</i> and is subtitled.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Antonello Belluco's 2006 <i>Antonio guerriero di Dio</i> or <i>Anthony, Warrior of God</i><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Biographical_film" title="Biographical film">biopic</a> about the saint.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Pedro_Rodrigues" title="João Pedro Rodrigues">João Pedro Rodrigues</a> directed the 2016 film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Ornithologist_(film)" title="The Ornithologist (film)">The Ornithologist</a></i>, a sort of modern-day fantastic allegory of the life of St. Anthony.</li></ul>
